/* Two bands: the identity band sizes itself to the thumbnail, the log takes
   everything that is left. Nothing is centred and nothing is free-floating --
   at 1080p that is the difference between a filled screen and a card adrift
   in the middle of one. */
.top{display:flex;flex:none;padding:26px 0 22px}
.det{flex:1;min-width:0;margin-left:30px;display:flex;flex-direction:column}
/* Width fixed, height NOT: with no height of its own the flex row's default
   align-items:stretch makes it exactly as tall as the details beside it, so the
   two columns keep lining up now that the stage band can grow the right one.
   min-height is the floor for a server whose name fits on one line. */
#thumb{position:relative;flex:none;width:480px;min-height:270px;border:1px solid #171b23;background:#0b0d12;overflow:hidden}
/* ABSOLUTE, not in flow. #thumb has no height of its own any more, so an
   in-flow image would be the thing that decides it -- and asset://mapimage is
   SQUARE, which blew the panel out to 480x480 and left the stage band floating
   in the middle of the gap it made. Out of flow it cannot size the box, and
   object-fit:cover crops it to whatever the details column ended up being. */
#thumb img{position:absolute;top:0;right:0;bottom:0;left:0;width:100%;height:100%;
 object-fit:cover;opacity:0;transition:opacity .5s ease}

/* The band between the title and the sheet is dead space for most of a connect:
   the sheet is pinned to the bottom of the thumbnail and the title above it is
   one or two lines, so at 1080p that left a palm-sized hole in the middle of
   the screen. It is filled with the one thing that has a value from the very
   first frame -- where the load actually IS -- and it takes exactly what is
   left over (flex:1), whatever the server name wrapped to. */
/* NOT .tl -- that one is already the thumbnail's top-left corner bracket, and
   reusing it silently restyled the corners AND made every rule here apply to a
   9px absolutely-positioned box.
   flex:1 with no min-height override on purpose: the default min-height:auto is
   what stops the four rows being squeezed to nothing when the sheet below wraps
   to more lines than the thumbnail is tall (a long gamemode label does that). It
   grows the panel past the thumbnail instead, and the log gives up the room. */
.stg{flex:1;margin-top:18px;display:flex;flex-direction:column;justify-content:center}
.stg .s{display:flex;align-items:baseline;padding:5px 0}

/* Everything above the log is content-sized and the log is the only thing that
   flexes, so on a 720p or windowed game the log is what gets crushed -- down to
   a single line, with the sheet above it clipping its own values. This is the
   whole layout stepping down a size rather than one panel absorbing the loss.
   A media query and not JS: it has to be right on the first painted frame. */
/* The other direction: a 1440p or 4K game hands the page that many CSS pixels,
   and every size here is in px -- so the bigger the screen, the smaller the
   whole thing looks, marooned inside a 1460px column. One step up past 1800px
   keeps it roughly the same apparent size instead. */

<script>
(function(){
var T0=Date.now(),need=0,done=0,total=0,seq=0,step=-1,stamp=[],DASH="
function E(id){return document.getElementById(id)}
function S(v){return String(v==null?"":v)}
function two(n){return(n<10?"0":"")+n}
function mmss(s){s=Math.max(0,Math.round(s));return Math.floor(s/60)+":"+two(s%60)}
function set(id,v){var e=E(id);if(e)e.textContent=(v===""||v==null)?DASH:v}
// One 1 Hz timer for everything that ticks. The rate is deliberately the plain
// average over the whole session, not a smoothed instantaneous one: the file
// stream is bursty enough that a windowed figure just flickers.
function up(){
 var d=new Date();
 set("clock",two(d.getHours())+":"+two(d.getMinutes())+":"+two(d.getSeconds()));
 var el=(Date.now()-T0)/1000;
 set("elapsed",mmss(el));
 if(done>0&&el>.75){
  var r=done/el;
  set("rate",(r<10?r.toFixed(1):Math.round(r))+"/s");
  set("eta",need>done?mmss((need-done)/r):"0:00");
function bar(){
 var b=E("bar");
 if(need>0){
  b.className="";
  var p=Math.max(0,Math.min(100,done*100/need));
  E("fill").style.width=p+"%";
  set("pct",Math.round(p)+"%");
  set("files",done+" / "+need);
  set("d1",done+" / "+need+" files");
 }else{
  b.className="idle";
// The four rows in the middle band. A stage that has been reached keeps its
// timestamp for the rest of the connect -- the tracker below is monotonic, so a
// row never goes back to pending and a time never moves.
function marks(){
 for(var i=0;i<4;i++){
  var e=E("s"+i);
  if(!e)continue;
  e.className="s"+(i<step?" done":(i==step?" on":""));
  if(i<=step&&!stamp[i]){
   stamp[i]=mmss((Date.now()-T0)/1000);
   set("w"+i,stamp[i]);
function log(tag,txt){
 var ol=E("log"),ph=E("ph");
 if(ph)ol.removeChild(ph);
 var last=ol.lastChild;
 if(last&&last.className)last.className=last.className.replace(" n","");
 var li=document.createElement("li");
 li.className=(tag=="dl"?"dl":"sys")+" n";
 li.innerHTML='<span class="ix m"></span><span class="tg"></span><span class="tx"></span><span class="ts m"></span>';
 seq++;
 li.childNodes[0].textContent=("00"+seq).slice(-3);
 li.childNodes[1].textContent=tag;
 li.childNodes[2].textContent=txt;
 li.childNodes[3].textContent=mmss((Date.now()-T0)/1000);
 ol.appendChild(li);
 // The panel clips the top itself; this only keeps the node count bounded on a
 // server that streams a few thousand files.
 while(ol.childNodes.length>40)ol.removeChild(ol.firstChild);
 set("logn",seq);
// Monotonic: GMod's status line jumps around (a downloaded .bsp says "map" in
// the same breath as "downloading"), so a stage that has been reached is never
// given back, and the download keywords win over the file name they carry.
function stage(s){
 s=" "+S(s).toLowerCase();
 var n=-1;
 if(/download|workshop|fastdl|extract|http/.test(s))n=1;
 else if(/lua|gamemode|client info|spawn|initializ|full update/.test(s))n=3;
 else if(/map|level|world|precach|resourc/.test(s))n=2;
 else if(/connect|retriev|server info|challenge|auth|ticket/.test(s))n=0;
 if(n<=step)return;
 step=n;
 var u=E("steps").getElementsByTagName("u");
 for(var i=0;i<u.length;i++)u[i].className=i<step?"done":(i==step?"on":"");
 marks();
// 76561197960265728 + Y*2 + X and back, all as decimal STRING arithmetic: a
// SteamID64 is past 2^53, so +/- on a JS number silently rounds it, and BigInt
// is not worth depending on inside an embedded browser.
function addBig(dec,n){
 var out="",c=n;
 for(var i=dec.length-1;i>=0;i--){
  var s=(+dec.charAt(i))+(c%10);
  c=Math.floor(c/10);
  if(s>9){s-=10;c++}
  out=s+out;
 while(c>0){out=(c%10)+out;c=Math.floor(c/10)}
 return out;
// a - b, or null when b is the bigger one (i.e. not an individual account id).
function subBig(a,b){
 while(b.length<a.length)b="0"+b;
 if(a.length<b.length)return null;
 var out="",bor=0;
 for(var i=a.length-1;i>=0;i--){
  var d=(+a.charAt(i))-(+b.charAt(i))-bor;
  if(d<0){d+=10;bor=1}else{bor=0}
  out=d+out;
 if(bor)return null;
 return out.replace(/^0+(?=\d)/,"");
// floor(dec / 2), long division so the string stays exact.
function halfBig(dec){
 var out="",carry=0,started=false;
 for(var i=0;i<dec.length;i++){
  var v=carry*10+(+dec.charAt(i)),q=v>>1;
  carry=v-q*2;
  if(q||started){out+=q;started=true}
 return out||"0";
// GMod hands the page whatever the engine had, and on this build that is the
// DECIMAL 64-bit id -- not STEAM_0:X:Y. Reading only the textual form was why
// the 64 landed in the "steam id" row and the "steam64" row stayed a dash. Both
// forms are accepted now and the missing half is derived, so neither row is
// empty while the other one has a value. Returns [textual, sixtyfour].
function ids(raw){
 var s=S(raw).replace(/^\s+|\s+$/g,"");
 if(!s)return ["",""];
 var m=/^STEAM_[0-5]:([01]):(\d+)$/i.exec(s);
 if(m)return [s.toUpperCase(),addBig("76561197960265728",(+m[2])*2+(+m[1]))];
 if(/^\d{15,20}$/.test(s)){
  var d=subBig(s,"76561197960265728");
  // Not an individual account: below the base, or an account number that does
  // not fit the 32 bits one has (a game server id, a clan or lobby id). There
  // is no STEAM_0:X:Y for those, so say so instead of printing a wrong one.
  if(d===null||d.length>10||+d>=4294967296)return ["not an account id",s];
  return ["STEAM_0:"+(+d.charAt(d.length-1)%2)+":"+halfBig(d),s];
 return [s,""];
window.GameDetails=function(servername,serverurl,mapname,maxplayers,steamid,gamemode,volume,lang,gamemodeNice){
 var nm=S(servername)||"Server",mp=S(mapname);
 set("sname",nm);
 document.title=nm;
 set("gmnice",S(gamemodeNice)||S(gamemode)||"unknown");
 set("gmraw",S(gamemode));
 set("mapname",mp);
 set("mapname2",mp);
 set("slots",(+maxplayers>0)?(+maxplayers)+" slots":"");
 set("lang",S(lang).toUpperCase());
 set("vol",Math.round((+volume||0)*100)+"%");
 var id=ids(steamid);
 set("sid",id[0]);
 set("sid64",id[1]);
 // Argument 2 is the server's loading URL, which is exactly what our hook
 // replaced -- so it carries the connect address instead. The page's own
 // {{ADDR}} was filled in when the panel opened, before the net channel
 // existed, so this is usually the first time the address is knowable at all.
 var su=S(serverurl);
 if(su&&!/^(https?|asset):/i.test(su)){set("addr",su);set("d0",su)}
 set("d2",mp);
 set("d3",S(gamemodeNice)||S(gamemode));
 if(mp){
  var u="asset://mapimage/"+mp,im=E("mapimg");
  im.onload=function(){E("thumb").className="ok";var b=E("bg");b.style.backgroundImage="url('"+u+"')";b.className="on"};
  im.onerror=function(){E("thumb").className="na"};
  im.src=u;
 stage("connect");
 log("net",nm);
window.SetStatusChanged=function(status){
 var s=S(status);
 set("status",s);
 // GMod puts the transferred volume in the status line itself ("... (4.2 MB
 // total) ..."); nothing else on the JS side ever reports bytes.
 var m=/\(([^()]*?)\s*total\)/i.exec(s);
 if(m&&m[1])set("data",m[1]);
 stage(s);
 log("sys",s);
window.DownloadingFile=function(file){
 var f=S(file);
 done++;
 set("status",f);
 stage("download");
 log("dl",f);
 bar();
// The count is what is still MISSING at that moment, and it is re-sent as the
// download table grows -- so the total is the high-water mark of what has been
// fetched plus what is left, never the last number on its own.
window.SetFilesNeeded=function(n){
 var left=+n||0;
 if(left+done>need){need=left+done;log("sys",need+" files to transfer")}
 bar();
window.SetFilesTotal=function(n){
 total=+n||0;
 set("content",total?total+" files":"");
 if(total&&need<=0)set("d1",total+" files listed");
var d0=new Date();
set("started",two(d0.getHours())+":"+two(d0.getMinutes()));
// {{ADDR}} is only there when the net channel already existed when the page was
// written (a changelevel, a retry); on a first connect it is a dash and the
// address arrives later, through GameDetails.
var ae=E("addr"),a0=ae?ae.textContent:"";
if(a0&&a0!=DASH)set("d0",a0);
up();
setInterval(up,1000);
})();

local K = "
local ST = rawget(_G, K)
if type(ST) ~= "table" then ST = {} rawset(_G, K, ST) end
ST.on = 
ST.url = "
if type(GameDetails) == "function" and GameDetails ~= ST.wrap then
	ST.orig = GameDetails
	ST.wrap = function(servername, serverurl, mapname, maxplayers, ...)
		if ST.on then
			local u = ST.url
			if type(u) == "string" and #u >= 5 then
				local openable = (u:sub(1, 4) == "http" or u:sub(1, 8) == "asset://")
				local mp = tonumber(maxplayers) or 0
				local cv = GetConVar and GetConVar("cl_enable_loadingurl")
				if openable and mp > 1 and cv and cv:GetBool() then
					serverurl = u
				else
					pcall(function()
						local pnl = GetLoadPanel and GetLoadPanel()
						if pnl and IsValid(pnl) then pnl:ShowURL(u, true) end
					end)
					serverurl = ""
				end
			else
				serverurl = ""
			end
		end
		return ST.orig(servername, serverurl, mapname, maxplayers, ...)
	end
	GameDetails = ST.wrap
if type(ST.wrap) ~= "function" then return "GameDetails not found" end
return "ok"

##circle_thickness
ESP Box
AABB (collision)
Legacy (0.55)
Silhouette (hitboxes)
Box size
AABB - the player's collision box, axis-aligned, all 8 corners
projected, then thinned. Follows crouching and model size.
Legacy - head and feet only, width = 55%% of height.
Silhouette - the model itself: every hitbox corner, measured across
your view axis. Tight to the actual model, exact at any angle, and
nothing about it is a tuned constant. Costs one bone setup per player.
Applies to every player: the whole element layout anchors to this rect.

##hbar_thickness

%.1f px
##hbar_rounding
Corner radius of the track. 0 = square ends, which is what lines
up with a square ESP box - rounded tips read as "not flush" no
matter how exact the length is.
This used to be forced to ~2.5px with no way out; the old look is
2.5 on this slider.
Length trim
##hbar_lentrim
Shaves this many pixels off EACH end of the bar.
The box's stroke reach and its rounded corners are already
accounted for; this is for the last pixel that depends on how
a given box style reads. Negative makes the bar overhang.
Background##hbar_bg
The dark plate under the fill - the bar's version of a text
shadow. Off leaves only the fill (and the outline, if it is on),
which is what you want over a dark model or right against the box.
Background color
##hbar_bgcol
Armor number
Health number
Only when damaged
On bar
Auto (outer side)
Above
Below
Number position
##hbar_textside
Default keeps the old behaviour: on the track when the bar is
vertical, outside it when horizontal.
Auto puts it outside on the side the bar is anchored to, so it

##qs_inv_ignore
Weapons to leave out, separated by , or ;
Each fragment is matched against the display name AND the
class, case-insensitively and anywhere inside them 
"physgun" hides it whichever of the two is being printed.
A fragment with * or ? is a mask over the WHOLE string:
  ttt_*   starts with     *knife   ends with
  *aug*   anywhere        p?stol   one any char
Careful: "ttt_" hits weapon_ttt_knife, "ttt_*" does not 
the class starts with weapon_. Bare fragments are safer.
e.g. physgun, camera, keys, pocket
##qs_inv_watch
The guns worth knowing about 
 traitor weapons, a knife,
whatever this gamemode makes dangerous. Same syntax as Ignore:
anywhere inside name or class, and * / ? mask the whole string.
e.g. knife, ttt_, c4, awp
Only these##qs_inv

list holds only those guns.
Off: the whole inventory prints and the listed ones are coloured.
An empty highlight list leaves this off 
 it never blanks the element.

##qs_disguise

i.e. when the player is disguised (UnionTeams / JCT keep
ply:Team() honest and only rewrite the job).
Show real team - the long-standing behaviour: reveal what he is.
Show job -> real - both, so the lie stays readable too.
Ignore - never swap, always print the job.

##qs_jobmatch
How job and team names are compared.
Exact - case-insensitive equality.
Contains - either string inside the other. Use this when the
server decorates jobs ("Job [VIP]" against a team named "Job"),
which under Exact makes EVERY player read as disguised.
Drag to snap zones. Drag again inside a zone to reorder.

##aim_v4_left

##aim_enabled_popup
aim_enabled
Enable aimbot
ON (default, the way the cheat always behaved): your real WASD is
put back into the command after the gamemode's Lua CreateMove hook
ran, so a server that zeroes movement there - handcuffs, stun,
arrest, roleplay freezes - does not hold you.
OFF: those locks work again, like a client with no cheat loaded.
Does not touch upmove: vehicle addons ship their aim distance in
that field, and overriding it makes mounted guns fire straight up.
override_movement_locks
Override movement locks
aim_players
Aim at players

Always on aim
After the locked target dies, stop aiming.
Hold-key mode: release and re-press the aim key to aim again.
Always-on aim: resumes after a short pause instead of
instantly snapping to the next player.

##anti_ac_v4_popup
anti_ac
Anti-AC protection
REQUIRED: Offsetting angles avoids immediate ban
from angle-consistency check.
aa_reversal
Anti-reversal
CRITICAL: If disabled, cheating/silent angles will NOT match
real mouse inputs, causing a 100% ban for Viewangle Manipulation!
aa_mouse
Fake mouse deltas
RECOMMENDED: Prevents autoshooting during rapid snaps
to avoid detection by snap checks.
aa_snap
Snap protection
CRITICAL: LSAC flags a tick delta of 0 or above 3 between two
consecutive commands while holding fire. This walks into the
shift inside that band instead of jumping, so backtrack keeps
working; unavoidable jumps are rate-limited to one per 3s.
aa_tickshift
Pace backtrack tickshift
CRITICAL: If disabled, clicking at identical intervals
will trigger a 100% ban for Autofire Timed Checks!
aa_timing
Autofire timing
Paces autofire/autoshoot by game time (not frames) so Nova
autoclick checks do not flag it. Needs Anti-AC on; if off,
high FPS can cause detection.
aa_nova
Nova autoclick bypass
Keeps opt-in game-event subscriptions off the wire. Only
Loggers > DamageLogs > "Subscribe to player_hurt event"
uses one today: it sets a bit in clc_ListenEvents, which
LSAC's sourcenet can majority-vote across players.
Off = the subscription is allowed while Anti-AC stays on.
aa_events
Block event subscriptions
target_priority
Target priority
##hitscan_perf_popup
Both walk the model's hitbox set: one visibility trace per hitbox
per target, per tick. Ordered takes the highest-priority visible
hitbox (head first); Closest takes the visible one nearest your
crosshair. Use the popup to trim the cost on a wide FOV.
hitbox_mode
Hitscan mode
Performance
Remembers each aim point's visibility for the current tick, so the
same point is not traced again by another backtrack record, the
ESP pass or the close-range head retry.
opt_vis_cache
Cache visibility traces
The two extra head points (for heads peeking cover) cost two traces
per target. ON = only within 800 units, where they actually help.
opt_multipoint_near
Head multipoint only close
Through cover
Keeps a target whose direct ray is blocked when the weapon pack's
own penetration maths says the bullet still arrives.
Supported packs: ArcCW, ARC9, TacRP, SWCS, TFA (incl. Insurgency),
M9K, CW 2.0, SWB (incl. the UT fork), FA:S 2.0. Each one is
modelled separately - budgets, material tables and damage loss
are that pack's own numbers, not a shared guess.
Silent on a pack with no wallbang, and on a server that has turned
its pack's penetration convar off.
Homigrad/Z-City has its own, older autowall with its own switch
under Compensation > Gamemode support - that pack ricochets and
fires from the world model's muzzle, so it needs its own simulator.
aim_autowall
Autowall
Damage that has to survive the cover before the shot is worth
taking. A round that arrives with nothing left only gives your
position away.
Ignored on a weapon whose damage could not be read - an unknown
number is not a reason to refuse a shot the maths says lands.
##v4_aw_dmg
Min damage
##v4_aw_walls
Walls
How many surfaces the bullet may cross.
Each wall is a long trace plus an exit search, run per aim point
per target - 1 is the honest default. CW 2.0, SWB and FA:S allow
exactly one and cap this themselves; SWCS allows four, ARC9
three, TFA whatever its weapon says.
Aim point
The server rewinds PLAYERS when it traces your bullet, and nothing
else. A body that is not a player - the prop_ragdoll a downed
Homigrad player actually is, or an NPC - is hit where it stands NOW,
while you aim where it was drawn: cl_interp + ping ago. Moving
bodies miss, still ones do not.
On = shift the aim point by that body's measured velocity. Players
are never touched, so backtrack keeps working as it does today.
lead_uncomped
Lead uncompensated bodies
Ceiling on the shift, in units. A tumbling ragdoll stops being
described by a straight line past some magnitude - this makes it
under-lead instead of walking the aim point off the body.
%.0f u

##nvr_v4_popup
comp_recoil
No visual recoil
Mode##nvr_v4
Classic (lock): for SetEyeAngles-recoil packs (M9K etc.) - freezes
  the view for 4 ticks after each shot so the kick never happens.
  Undetectable (zero angle delta), but the mouse stiffens briefly
  while firing, and view-punch packs are not covered.
Rage: both recoil families, live mouse, no screen movement.
  Kills the SetEyeAngles kick by dropping view writes that come
  from inside prediction (the mouse never writes from there), and
  compensates the view punch on the sent angles while you hold
  fire, so ARC9/HL2-style bullets land on the crosshair.
Keep the visual kick on screen while the bullets stay compensated.
Looks like a legit player pulling down - for OBS/spectating admins.
nvr_v4_showpunch
Show screen recoil
LSAC servers only: drops IN_ATTACK on 1-3 cooldown ticks per shot
so the Eye Sync forcing re-anchors to our angles instead of letting
the spray climb. Costs attack edges (~fire rate, randomized) and
does nothing while ADS (IN_ATTACK2) is held.
nvr_v4_eyesync
Eye Sync bypass
##backtrack_v4_popup
comp_backtrack
Backtrack
%d ms
##v4_backtrack
Backtrack window
Marks every stored record (each backtrackable position).
Purely visual - turning it off changes nothing about the aim.
Also covers the records HVH lag comp stores.
Style picks what a record is drawn AS; see the tooltip there.
backtrack_visualize
Show records
Style##bt_vis_style
Dots  - a marker at each record's eye. Says where and how many, reads at a glance, hides nothing.
Chams - each record drawn in the POSE the server would restore. Says what a shot solved against it would actually hit, which is the view that answers 'why did that miss'. One full model draw per pose.
Both  - the dot marks the record its pose belongs to.
##bt_vis_count2
Poses shown
Hide records outside the server's acceptance band.
Those are poses it will never rewind to, so drawing one
claims a shot that cannot happen.
Off shows the whole stored history, shootable or not.
backtrack_vis_valid
Only shootable ones
Writes cl_extrapolate 0 straight onto the ConVar (it is a cheat
cvar, so the console cannot set it on a normal server).
When the connection starves, the engine runs EVERY entity up to
0.25s past its last real position - a pose the server never had,
so both the live aim and the records taken then are fiction.
With this on it clamps to the last real snapshot instead.
Costs a visible stutter during packet loss. Does NOT affect
fakelaggers: the engine already clamps a player whose updates
stopped while other packets keep arriving.
Restored on disconnect and on unload.
backtrack_no_extrap
No extrapolation
Acknowledges an OLDER server packet than the one you really got,
so the server measures a bigger round trip and widens its
lag-compensation window by exactly that much.
Nothing you send is delayed - there is no input lag. The cost is
that your ping is genuinely higher to everyone, scoreboard included.
Remote servers only: a listen server has no latency to inflate.
backtrack_fakeping
Fake ping
##fakeping_target
Target ping
TOTAL latency the server should measure, real ping included.
The cap is 890 and not 1000 because the server prunes its
history at sv_maxunlag (1.0s) and the pose it must still find
sits at this plus your interp.
capped at %.0f ms (interp %.0f + real ping %.0f)
Rewind depth is your ping + this + interp, and sv_maxunlag caps the SUM.
Past the cap the depth stops growing but the acceptance band keeps
climbing, so every usable record ends up jammed against the oldest
edge of the history -- exactly the poses the server is dropping.
That reads as 'backtrack misses'.
The request is clamped here rather than sent, so this costs you no
extra real ping either. Lower the slider to make the line go away.
rewind %.0f ms (+%.0f ms interp)
+%d of %d ms (ramping up)
+%d ms (server sees %d ms)
Never acknowledge across a reliable-state change.
Costs a little depth right after chat/kill traffic.
Leave on unless you are debugging.
fakeping_strict
Strict reliable safety
Also rewinds the reliable flip-flop. Ages the ack clock
harder, but the server then RETRANSMITS reliable traffic -
chat, kill feed and joins arrive twice. Off by default.
fakeping_rewind_reliable
Rewind reliable state
Simulates your next movement tick locally so the aimbot leads with
the origin you are about to have. It runs with the gamemode's Lua
hooks off, so GM:SetupMove / GM:Move never see it.
Turn OFF on gamemodes that restrict movement from Lua - handcuffs,
stuns, cutscene locks. There the sim moves you when the server does
not: you walk while cuffed and then rubber-band back.
No spread is not affected either way.
gm_v4_movesim
Local movement prediction

gm_v4_zcity_kr
Kill recoil (screen)
Zeroes Primary.Spread, the pack's real bullet cone.
Only the 13 shotgun / muzzleloader classes have one.
CLIENT ONLY. The server runs the same Lua off its own

but the damage still uses the server's cone. There is no
way to reach that cone from a client 
 its seed counts
every bullet fired on the server.
Needs No spread on.
gm_v4_zcity_kc

gm_v4_zcity_rag
Aim at ragdoll body
Reverts the whole Z-City aim path to build 16 (10.08) in one
switch: no per-tick muzzle sample, no shot feedback loop, no
walk lead, the old 14 deg solver ceiling, the raw view basis,
no bullet-redirect detection, and the pack's norecoil flag
written unconditionally. Also restores the unconditional
CreateMove angle restore that 7b2bccf made conditional.
Overrides Pack switch and Bullet redirect while it is on.
For comparing against 16 directly. If 16 comes back, turn the
parts below back on one at a time to find which one cost it.
gm_v4_zcity_v16
Aim like build 16
The gun fires along its barrel, not your eye line.
Solver also corrects manual shots when Nospread is enabled.
gm_v4_zcity_muzzle
Muzzle comp
%.0f deg
##gm_v4_zcity_snap
Snap radius
Does nothing while Smooth is 0.
Inside this many degrees the aim stops smoothing and takes the
solution in one step.
The last few degrees are where the barrel model's own error
lives, and on Z-City the shot is gated on the REAL barrel at
the angle being sent - so crawling that stretch does not miss,
it holds the trigger for the whole approach.
0 turns it off. Bigger = a larger instant step on acquisition.
Off preserves the pack's shoot animation.
Auto also preserves it while Muzzle comp is Solver,
and uses the pack flag in other compensation modes.
Always disables the client animation even though the
server still runs it; this can make the muzzle model
disagree with the server, including on rifles.
Use Auto for the official pose solver.
gm_v4_zcity_pack
Pack switch
Auto keeps the barrel ray for the supported Homigrad contract.
IsVR alone never changes the ray method. Use Always only for
a custom base whose bullet code redirects toward the eye trace.
gm_v4_zcity_eye
Bullet redirect
AutoFire uses a fresh muzzle sample and the aim for this command.
Pistol recoil and hand movement already included in the
official pose calculation do not need to settle first.
Your own clicks are not blocked by this option.
gm_v4_zcity_fresh
Fire on fresh solve
Mode None only, Z-City only. Runs the aim once per tick,
the way Silent and PSilent do, instead of once per
rendered frame.
The barrel this pack aims by is sampled once a tick, so
per frame the aim solves several times against the same
sample and steers the view between the steps - that is
the crosshair shaking around the head.
Costs: with smoothing on, the crosshair walks at tick
rate instead of frame rate.
gm_v4_zcity_tickaim
Aim on tick
Stops the crosshair shaking on aim mode None.
The solved angle is written to your real view, that turns
the gun, and the gun's new position changes the next
solution - a loop that rings at a few Hz and looks like
the aimbot is picking random angles around the head.
This holds the barrel correction between ticks and only
re-takes it once the gun has stopped moving. Tracking is
not slowed: the angle to the target is still rebuilt every
frame, only the gun-offset part is held.
Silent and PSilent never move your view, so this does
nothing there.
gm_v4_zcity_hold
Steady barrel (aim None)
Shoot through thin cover when the bullet still gets there.

##rage_left
Rage aim
Enable##rage
Runs BESIDE the legit aimbot, reusing its target selection,
backtrack records, visibility and resolver -- only the policy
differs: no smoothing, no FOV cone, the angle is written on the
tick it exists.
This is loud. It is meant to be.
##rage_hotkey
Held, not toggled. Unbound (0) means always on while enabled.
Pull the trigger whenever the weapon backend reports ready.
There is no hitchance gate: with Nospread on the cone is no
longer random, so a 'chance' would be gating on a number that
stopped describing anything.
Write the angle into the command only. Off also moves your
actual view, which is visible to you and to spectators.
Extrapolate unreachable targets
For targets the server refuses to rewind (fake laggers). Carries them
forward through the real movement code, in steps of their own command
bundle, to a pose the server is actually holding.
Falls back to cl_lagcompensation 0, which the server can see.
Release anti-LSAC guards
Turns aimbot_settings.anti_lsac OFF while rage is held and puts
YOUR value back the moment it is not -- the angle-delta limiter,
the reversal check, the fake mouse deltas and the movement octant
snap all hang off that one master.
Every one of them exists to make the aim look like a hand, and
every one costs accuracy. In a rage fight the anti-aim has already
given that away.

##hvh_right
Resolver
Enable resolver
Mode##res_mode
Replays the enemy's animation for several guesses at the commands
we never received. Does nothing to a MOVING enemy -- the engine pins
his feet to his eyes, so there is nothing to resolve.
Never touches the yaw, only flattens a fake pitch. The safe choice
on servers full of legit players.
Safe point
Sample six points around each head/chest/stomach box instead of
trusting the centre and two diagonals.
THE CASE IT FIXES: the head is the chosen group and an arm is in
front of it. With three samples all three trace into the arm, the
head is called invisible, and the aimbot drops a group and shoots
the ARM. A head box is a few units across and a limb rarely covers
all of it -- this finds the exposed sliver and keeps the head.
Costs traces only when nothing on the preferred group is visible
yet: the scan still stops at the first point it can see, and the
centre stays first because it is the most forgiving of a wrong
resolve.
With Multi-hypothesis points also on, these extra samples are
ordered by how many resolver guesses contain them -- that is the
real safe point: one that hits whichever guess turns out right.
Multi-hypothesis points
Build a skeleton per guess and prefer aim points that hit under the
most of them. Live targets only, not backtrack records.
##res_opts
res_opts
Resolver internals
%d%%
##safepoint_scale
Safe point spread
How far the six samples sit from the box centre, as a percentage
of its half-size.
Low = huddled near the centre: safest against a wrong resolve, but
finds fewer exposed slivers. High = out at the faces: finds the
peeking edge, but a point near the face leaves the box the moment
the resolve is off. 70%% is the compromise.
##res_multipose_n
Hypotheses
How many guesses to build. Each one costs a bone setup.
Seed brute from body signals
Start the search at directions taken from the target (last hit,
movement heading, goal feet yaw) instead of a blind table.
Ignore uninformative shots
Do not move the search on an outcome that says nothing about the yaw.
Two cases, both measurable rather than guessed:
  * every hypothesis covered the aim point -- they all predict the SAME
    outcome, so a miss was not caused by the yaw and a hit does not name
    a winner (needs Multi-hypothesis points);
  * the target crouched mid-flight -- duck is predicted for you and only
    interpolated for him, so that pose was never the server's.
The search gets ONE scored shot per round trip. Spending those on
misses the yaw did not cause walks it away from the right angle exactly
as fast as it walks toward it. Off = the old behaviour, charge everything.
Pitch correction
Enemy sends pitch +-89 to tilt his whole skeleton. This flattens it
to 0 before the hitboxes are built.
Torso twist correction
Turning the legs alone leaves the chest and head at the twist the
client guessed. This sets the twist the server holds. Leave on.
Apply in animation state
Experimental: hook the animation state and let the engine rebuild
the pose instead of rotating it. Mostly covered by Torso twist now.
Debug readout
Per-enemy state next to each player: AA/legit verdict, choke,
deg/tick, current guess, misses and hits.
Feedback: player_hurt (exact)
Feedback: health poll (approx)
Where hit/miss comes from. The exact source needs
"Subscribe to player_hurt event" under Loggers > DamageLogs.
Visualize desync
Master switch for desync visualization.
Use ... to configure lines and fake chams separately.
##desync_visual_opts
desync_visual_opts
Desync visualization
Direction lines

Excluded from GTS and streamproof clean captures.
Rebuild your own model
Builds your third-person body at the yaw the SERVER is holding, so what you see is what an enemy's hitboxes are doing.
The engine builds the pose, so the torso twist and the limbs are the server's.
Off falls back to Fake chams, which rotates an already-built pose rigidly -- that is why the red copy looks stretched and bent.
Visual only: the server is driven by the command angles, which this never touches.
Fake chams
Ghost is off while the model is rebuilt.

lagcomp_opts
##lagcomp_offset
Tick offset
Fine-tune the sent tick_count if hits land just
ahead/behind the enemy. 0 = auto (ping+interp).
Raise if shots hit where they WERE, lower if ahead.
0 = auto (ping + interp).
Mode##lagcomp_mode
Disable interpolation##no_interp
cl_interpolate 0 
 stops YOUR client drawing enemies a lerp behind,
while cl_interp keeps REPORTING the same interp to the server, so
its rewind is unchanged. That split is the whole trick.
Tick-quantised animation##tick_anims
Advances client animation once per TICK, from CL_Move, by exactly
one tick 
 the way the server did it 
 instead of once per render
frame by the frame time. Backtrack then records poses the server
actually held, not the interpolation between two of them.
Touches the animation clock of every animating entity, so if
animation stutters or freezes anywhere, this is the switch.
UNTESTED.

##chams_opts
chams_opts
Mode##player_chams_mode

Outline: a coloured border around the character and/or the real

Material##player_chams_mat
Opacity
%.2f
##chams_alpha
1.00 = the solid chams as before.
Lower makes the fill see-through - the transparent chams look.
Works with any material; Flat and Additive ghost read best.
Note: the viewmodel chams draw from the same material list, so with the
same look selected there they turn see-through too.
Not mounted on this install - drawing Flat.
Refract material: distorts what is BEHIND the model, so the ESP-editor preview can only approximate it. In game it looks right.
Outline##glow_outline
The coloured border around the character.
Show model through walls##glow_xray
Redraws the player's own textured model with the depth suppressed,
so you see the actual body behind geometry. Independent of the border:
turn one of the two off for outline-only or model-only.
Fill model with material##glow_fillmodel
Draws the model through walls repainted with a chams material -
the same look list "Solid colors" uses - with its own colour and opacity.
Independent of "Show model through walls", which keeps the real texture:
with both on, this one is drawn on top.
Fill material##glow_fill_mat
Fill color##glow_fill_col
One colour for the whole fill - it is drawn through walls, so there is no
visible/hidden split to make here. The border keeps its own two colours.
Outline through walls##glow_thr
Colours the part of the body that is behind geometry too, in the
"hidden" colour. Off = the border only marks what you can actually see.
Sharp border (no glow)##glow_sharp
No blur at all. The silhouette is grown by a fixed number of
pixels and its inside is cut out, so the border is hard-edged and evenly
thick instead of fading. Overrides "Soft glow".
Soft glow##glow_soft
On: the blur bounces through the engine's quarter-size bloom buffer, which
smears the ring far wider than its radius - a soft halo.
Off: full-resolution blur, so a small radius leaves a crisp border
and no glow at all. Pair "off" with thickness 1-2.
Hidden##glow_tr_col
Visible##glow_vis_col
The border is coloured PER PIXEL: the part of the body behind geometry
gets the "hidden" colour, the part in line of sight the "visible" one.
Thickness (px)
##glow_thick
Border width in screen pixels - an exact count, not a falloff.
##glow_size
Blur radius = how far the ring spreads from the silhouette.
~2 for a crisp border, ~5 and up with "Soft glow" on.
##glow_passes
Extra blur passes - widens the ring further. Keep at 1 for a crisp border.

##worldtint_hotkey
Observer bypass
Stream-proof view

Renders the scene TWICE: a clean first-person pass for the stream, then the cheat view for you.

##sp_opts
sp_opts
Clean pass##sp_mode
Offscreen (v5): the clean pass renders into a private render target.
Works with threaded rendering, so your gmod_mcore_test stays as-is.
Recommended -- this is the mode that keeps your FPS.
Backbuffer (v4): renders both passes into the screen buffer and
snapshots it mid-frame. That only works with SYNCHRONOUS rendering,
so it needs "gmod_mcore_test 0" in console -- set by YOU, we never
touch the cvar. While mcore is 1 this mode simply skips the clean
pass (nothing is published to OBS; your own view is unaffected).
Backbuffer mode needs "gmod_mcore_test 0" in console.
Capture UI panels (Q menu, F1/F4)
Also paint UI-panel vgui frames into the OBS capture: Q spawn menu,
F1/F4 gamemode menus, context menu, RSC pop-ups.
Off = only in-game HUD panels (hp/ammo, scoreboard, chat, net_graph).
Trade-off: if you open the Escape (pause) menu while streaming, it will
appear in the OBS capture too. Leave ON unless that matters.
Hide clean pass from Lua
##sp_lua_hide_opts
sp_lua_hide_opts
Extended skip
Lifecycle hooks (PreRender / PostRender / RenderScene)
Also skip Lua frame-lifecycle hooks (PreRender / PostRender / RenderScene)
in the clean pass. These go through the SIMPLE dispatch which has no
bracket calls around it, so skipping is verified-safe.
even here: PreDrawSkyBox, PreDrawOpaqueRenderables, PreDrawViewModel
etc. Those go through CallN which wraps the dispatch in engine bracket calls
Turn this ON if a server counts PreRender/RenderScene in addition to HUD hooks.
RenderScreenspaceEffects (risky)
The last hook a per-frame counter can see doubling.
CallN
Clean frame loses Lua screen-space effects.
Scene draw hooks (risky)
Opaque / translucent / viewmodel / player draw hooks.
Same CallN risk. The clean frame also loses whatever the
SERVER draws in them, so a screengrab looks different.
%d Hz
##sp_clean_hz
Clean-pass FPS
How often the clean stream frame is re-rendered. Match it to your OBS/record FPS.
Lower = cheaper (the heavy 2nd render runs this many times/sec instead of every game frame).
This is the main FPS lever: at 30-60 Hz the double-render runs far less often than your game FPS.
##sp_clean_scale
Clean-pass quality
Resolution of the clean stream pass, as a %% of your screen (Offscreen mode only).
Lower = the 2nd render draws fewer pixels = more FPS if you are GPU/fill-bound,
at the cost of a softer stream image (upscaled back to full size for OBS/RSC).
100%% = identical to your screen. Text/HUD in the stream get blurry below ~70%%.
Below 100%% the clean target also gets a size no engine render target shares,
which makes the OBS bridge pick it up on the first frame instead of retrying.
##fb_hotkey
Enable##fb
Client-side world lighting.
Light = brightness boost (ColorModulate) with a slider.
FullBright = true unlit world (selfillum on world brushes), lights dark corners too.
Cheap = native render.SetLightingMode: touches no materials, no FPS cost, instant on/off.
White LM = replaces the map lightmap with white while preserving textures and World Tint.
Config Hook = forces nFullbright only inside the engine's normal OverrideConfig call.
Modes 0-3 are cvar-free and hidden from clean captures; Config Hook is not.
fb_mode
%.1fx
##fb_bright
render.SetLightingMode(1) vs (2).
2 over-brightens further; the engine clamps anything above.
fb_cheap_lvl
Level
##fb_white_lm_intensity
Intensity
Brightness of the replacement white lightmap.
0.45-0.65 usually preserves texture contrast while removing dark areas.

Dormant##ent
Keeps the box after the server stops sending the entity.

drop distant entities from the client entirely - the box then
freezes at the last position it was seen at, dimmed.
Exact for static props like money printers; anything that
moves while untransmitted is drawn where you last saw it.

Refresh##nv
Release every cached table reference and walk the paths again.
Needed when an addon replaces the table object itself:
a type check cannot see that, the replacement is still a table.
Scan##nv
Walk _G looking for tables that hold per-player data.
A table counts only if connected players are actually
found inside it, so config arrays do not show up.
Fields##nv
nv_fields
Point the BUILT-IN elements (Nickname, Usergroup, Team/Job)
at your own template. Configure a server once here instead of
cloning a Netvar element into every layout.
The override feeds the JOB side of both job elements; the team side
always comes from the networked team, so disguise detection keeps
working whatever you put here.
Leave a field empty to keep the built-in behaviour.

anything that disagrees.

Reads NW updates off the WIRE instead of from Lua: verifies

SWEP Lua calls (log)##se_probe
Logs each (weapon class, Lua function) pair the first time a
SWEP dispatches it 
 one line per pair for the whole session,
not per call.
What it is for: FireBullets already tells us the class, the
cone and primary-vs-secondary for packs that shoot through the
engine. The packs that never reach it 
 ARC9's Lua cone, CLHR
and Ley with client-side hitreg 
 leave no native trace that
PrimaryAttack ran at all. This is that trace, without putting
any Lua on the machine.
Nothing consumes it and no return value is changed; every call
is relayed untouched.

Repair join
Press after a server refused the join with
"Steam validation rejected".
Checks that Steam has given the account a connect token back,
then drops the stale auth ticket the engine cached for that
server so the next connect asks for a fresh one.
If it says the pool is still empty, wait a few seconds and
press again.
Diagnose
Writes the full account report to the log: licence,
ownership ticket, connect tokens, running game - and WHO OWNS
the copy of the game. A borrowed (family-shared) copy is what
makes some servers let you in and then throw you out.
Free. Ctrl+click also spends one auth ticket and one connect
token on a live join probe.

Jump bug
Crouches through a fall and unducks + jumps on the one command
where the ground sits 36-38u below you: the engine hands out the
ground entity before it checks for a landing, so no fall damage is
ever computed - custom OnPlayerHitGround/GetFallDamage included.
The window is 2 units wide, so not every fall lines up; misses land
normally.

##warp_opts
warp_opts

##warp_ticks
Charge ticks
Cap at server limit
Auto-repeat while held
Over sv_maxusrcmdprocessticks the server drops
the backlog: 12 ticks = 216u, 32 = only 72u.
Cap keeps the charge just under it.
Tap mode (tap to arm, tap to teleport)
Hold mode: hold key to charge, release = teleport.
Tap mode: tap once to arm (auto-buffers), tap again
to teleport - no holding. Lower ticks = works on
more (strict) servers. Auto-recovers if a server
rejects the jump (no more freeze).
%d / %d ticks
Movement bank
What the server can still pay for. Refills one tick per tick,
spends one per command it processes. A warp is clamped to this,
not to the slider above.
requested %d  spent %d  starved %d
discarded bursts: %d (last %.0f%% of expected)
The server never tells us it threw a burst away -- our commands
were 'accepted' and the sequence advanced. This counts the only
evidence a discard leaves: the burst travelled far less than that
many ticks should have carried you.

Fakelag
##fakelag_opts
fakelag_opts
##fakelag_hotkey
##fakelag
Choked packets
##fakelag_variance
Variance
How far below the setting the depth may drop, re-rolled
once per cycle. 0%% means every packet gap is identical
forever, which no real connection produces.
Mode##fakelag_mode
Break lag compensation on rage shots
Lets the rage aim set cl_lagcompensation 0 for shots at a target
the server refuses to rewind, so the shot resolves against where
the target IS. Paired with rage's 'Extrapolate unreachable
targets' -- neither half is worth anything alone.
LOUD: cl_lagcompensation is FCVAR_USERINFO, so every flip is a
userinfo update the server can see. Flips are rate-limited to two
per second and always restored.
Flush on big moves
Send immediately once you have moved more than 64 units
within one choke cycle. The server refuses to rewind a player
across a jump that large, so choking through one throws away
your own backtrack records.
When to choke packets. Shot commands are
never choked (fakelag can't eat your shots).
Always: constant. While moving: only above
40 u/s. In air: only off the ground.
Peek: choke behind cover, flush on exposure.
Double tap
##dt_opts
dt_opts
##dt_hotkey
##dt_ticks
Shift ticks
On the shot, this many extra commands are sent.
They are full movement ticks, so the server runs
them at once and you also LEAP forward - fewer
ticks = shorter jump.
Hold the bind to arm it; unbound = every shot.
Over 14 one packet cannot hold them all (15 cmds
max), so the surplus rides the next one or is lost.

ConVar
cvar name...
##tools_cvar_name
value...
##tools_cvar_value
Set##tools_cvar_set
Console write, with the blocking flags cleared first.
Picks its own route: for sv_cheats / sv_allowcslua the console
cannot work and the flag unlock would crash the game, so Set
falls back to the direct object write on its own.
Read##tools_cvar_read
Unlock (clear blocking flags)##tools_cvar_flags
Set does this automatically 
 the button is here to unlock a
cvar without changing it. Clears CHEAT / DEVONLY / HIDDEN /
REPLICATED, which are what make the console throw the write
away. USERINFO is kept: removing it would stop the value ever
reaching the server.
sv_cheats and sv_allowcslua are refused: GMod checks their exact
flag word every entity packet and hard-Error()s if it changed.
Force value (never touches flags)##tools_cvar_force
Same write, different trade-off from Set.
Set clears CHEAT / DEVONLY / REPLICATED to get the command past
the console, and those stay cleared 
 a cvar whose flag word no
longer matches its registration is trivial for a server to
enumerate. Force writes the object directly and leaves every
flag exactly as the engine registered it.
Use Set normally. Use Force when the cvar should end up looking
untouched, or when you want a local-only value with no console
side effects.
Local either way for replicated cvars: the server keeps its own
value and can overwrite yours with NET_SetConVar.

##clean_opts
clean_opts
Run on game exit##clean_on_exit
Runs the selected file steps again when gmod.exe itself exits.
It is skipped for the cheat's normal unload, so unload/re-inject
is unaffected. SQLite and ConVars are not called through game APIs
from this path; database files are only deleted if no longer locked.
Files
Wipe data folder##clean_data
garrysmod/data, ALWAYS in full. The ban markers a server
writes there (vcmod_download_data*, stormfox/lightmap_*,
vcmod/settings_sh_*, aphone/link_imgur_*, ulx/ulx_log_*,
awarn3/client_id_*) carry the server's own random id in the
name, so there is nothing stable to pick out -- and the next
AC uses six other names anyway.
Legit addon settings go with them.
Downloads##clean_downloads
garrysmod/download, downloads and downloadlists --
every file servers made you fastdl.
Lua / Workshop cache##clean_cache
garrysmod/cache.
Crash dumps##clean_crashes
crashes/ plus loose *.mdmp / *.dmp next to the exe.
A dump written while the cheat was injected contains the
full loaded-module list -- our DLL by name and base address.
Disable lua/bin DLLs##clean_luabin
Binary Lua modules in garrysmod/lua/bin are a scored
indicator on their own. Renamed to *.dll.disabled, not
deleted -- rename them back to restore.
SQLite + ConVars
Wipe databases##clean_sql
We run as a DLL inside the ALREADY RUNNING game, so a .db the
process opened at startup can never be deleted from here.
What is possible is emptying it through the sqlite connection
that already owns it -- every table dropped, every cookie
deleted -- for cl.db and mn.db. No Lua is involved: the
engine's own sqlite3_exec and its database handle are found
by signature, and each connection is asked (PRAGMA
database_list) which file it just worked on.
sv.db is only open while YOU host, so it is deleted outright.
GMod's sqlite is built with SQLITE_OMIT_VACUUM: DROP TABLE
leaves the old bytes in the freed pages. The wipe therefore
refills the freelist with junk until PRAGMA freelist_count
hits 0. Reclaiming the SPACE still needs the game closed.
A .db is never truncated -- a zeroed file under a live sqlite
handle is a corrupt database, not a clean one.
Reset flagged ConVars##clean_convars
cl_timeout back to 30 (a banned client gets a secret value
stored in it) plus net_graph / developer / cl_showpos to 0.
Written natively, so a Lua RunConsoleCommand override never
sees it. Applied on the next tick in-game.
Fingerprint
Re-roll file times##clean_filetimes
Randomises the modification time of exactly the four paths
the fingerprint hashes:
  garrysmod/gameinfo.txt
  garrysmod/lua/autorun/base_vehicles.lua
  garrysmod/lights.rad
  <root>/sourceengine (the folder itself)
They are the only part of a device fingerprint the client
actually controls.
Re-roll install age##clean_install_age
Also ages every loose file in the game root (what
file.Find("*", "base_path") returns) back by months, so
"fresh install" / "reinstall" checks -- oldest file newer
than 1-2 days -- stop firing after a re-download.
scan..##clean_scan
Rescan##clean_scan
##clean_issues
Nothing flagged.
##clean_log
Everything off by default. A file the game holds open cannot
be deleted -- it gets emptied in place instead (never a .db:
those go through the sql wipe).
File cloak
Hide folders from Lua##fscloak
file.Find("addons/*") is the cheapest scan a server can run, and the
listing it returns is a fingerprint of this machine -- every addon you
have, every binary module in lua/bin, every leftover in between.
With this on, the paths in the list below answer as if they were not
there: gone from listings, and Exists / IsDir / Read / Open / Size /
Time answer the way they answer for a path that never existed. The
engine is untouched -- everything mounted stays mounted, only what
Lua is TOLD changes.
Does not cover require / include: pcall(include, path) is still an
existence oracle for a .lua file.
##fscloak_opts
fscloak_opts
Cloaked paths
Hidden = does not exist at all.
Empty  = the folder is still there, it is just never
         holding anything (right for addons/: the
         folder is stock, the contents are the tell).
*  matches within one folder, ** at any depth:
lua/*.lua hides loose Lua files sitting in lua/,
lua/**.lua hides every .lua under it.
##fscloak_list
Nothing cloaked. Add a path below.
##fsdel
empty##fsmode
hidden##fsmode
A pattern matches files, not a folder, so it is always hidden.
Click to switch this path between hidden and empty.
lua/bin, addons/x...
##fscloak_new
##fscloak_mode
Add##fscloak_add
Restore defaults##fscloak_defaults
Puts the built-in list back: addons (empty), lua/bin (hidden),
lua/*.lua (hidden).
The last one is precise on purpose -- a stock garrysmod/lua holds
only directories, so a loose .lua sitting there is always
something that was put there (a dump, an executor's scratch file).
A broad lua/* would swallow the stock folders instead and make the
whole tree vanish, which is far louder than the files it hides.
Replaces the current list, so a config saved with its own rules
gets the defaults back only when you press this.
Paths are relative to garrysmod/, and one rule covers every way
of spelling the same folder -- "lua/bin" catches all three of
file.Find("lua/bin/*","MOD"), file.Find("bin/*","LUA") and
file.Find("garrysmod/lua/bin/*","base_path").
Use data/... for the data folder, @root/... for what really is
outside garrysmod/ (bin/, sourceengine/, the exe).
Beyond the file library
Scrub Lua file paths (debug info)##fscloak_dbg
Every function carries the name of the file it came from, and
debug.getinfo / debug.traceback / jit.util.funcinfo hand it over
without touching a single file API. Walk hook.GetTable() and you
have a list of what is loaded on this client.
With this on, a function whose file matches a rule above reports
as a C function: source "=[C]", short_src "[C]", what "C",
line numbers -1 -- all of it together, because half a rewrite is
an impossible combination and a sharper tell than the leak.
Chunk names are spelled "lua/<addon-subfolder>/..." or
"addons/<name>/...", so the addons rule already covers the
second; add lua/<folder> for the first.
Trade-off: an addon of yours that looks up its OWN path this way
gets [C] too. Error message text is not covered - only these
three functions are.
Hide workshop addons (engine.GetAddons)##fscloak_ws
engine.GetAddons() returns your whole workshop subscription --
title, wsid, size, mounted -- and never goes near the filesystem,
so nothing above touches it. Entries whose .gma sits under a
cloaked path are dropped.
(!) With the default addons rule that empties the list, and some
gamemodes MOUNT CONTENT from it -- wOS dynabase (Z-City/Homigrad)
walks GetAddons and calls game.MountGMA(addon.file) for models.
Turn this on only if you can live without that.
Hide other mounted games (engine.GetGames)##fscloak_games
Reports every game except garrysmod as not installed and not
mounted. This is a pure fingerprint input -- nova hashes exactly
these flags into its device id.
The list itself is kept, only the flags are cleared: GMod's set of
known games is fixed, so an empty list is an anomaly while
"owns nothing but the base game" is the commonest setup there is.
Also the main-menu realm##fscloak_menu
Off by default: no anticheat runs in the menu realm, and GMod's own
save / dupe / demo browsers are file.Find callers there.
The realm you host on is never cloaked at all -- lying to your own
server would break hosting.

Live feed##dmg_feed
On-screen damage feed, top-left by default.
Drag it while the menu is open.

##dmg_feed_opts
dmg_feed_opts
Clear##dmg_clear
Subscribe to player_hurt event
Default (off): damage is read from the health netvar we already receive.
Passive, nothing is requested from the server, nobody can see it.
On: asks the server to send us its own player_hurt event -> each shotgun
pellet as its own hit, the attacker straight from the server, and damage
on players you cannot see (dormant / hidden HP).
Cost: the subscription sets one bit in our clc_ListenEvents. LSAC ships
sourcenet and majority-votes that bitfield across players, so it CAN spot
the odd one out. Pure-Lua anticheats cannot see it at all.
Blocked by Aimbot -> Anti-AC -> "Block Event Subscriptions" (on by
default); turn that sub-toggle off to keep this while Anti-AC stays on.
(blocked: Anti-AC events)
(live)
Visible lines
##dmg_feed_lines
Older lines are pushed out fast once the stack is full.
Hold time
##dmg_feed_life
How long a line stays before it fades out.
Merge repeat hits
Folds shots landing back to back on the same target
into one line ("120 x5") instead of one line per pellet.
Newest at bottom
Off: the newest line sits on top and older ones slide down.
On: lines are added under the stack, oldest on top.

Override while zoomed

##cam_vm_opts
cam_vm_opts

##cam_vm_fov
VM FOV
Custom aspect ratio
Render the view at an explicit width:height instead of the screen's own.
Narrower than the screen = stretched (models look wider), wider = squished.
Hidden from streamproof captures.
##cam_aspect_opts
cam_aspect_opts
Two-axis stretch
Two real, independent axes. A width:height pair
cannot do that -- the ratio is a single number,
so 16:9 and 32:18 render identically and both
fields move the same thing.
Legacy multiplier
The old control: one number scaling the ratio
the screen already has (>1 wider, <1 squished).
Off = explicit width:height, which comes out the
same on any monitor.
##cam_stretch_x
Horizontal
Sideways only (rides the FOV).
>1 = models wider, less visible to the sides. <1 = squished, more visible.
##cam_stretch_y
Up/down only (rides the ratio).
>1 = models taller, less visible above/below. <1 = squished, more visible.
Both axes equal = plain zoom, no stretch
wide
tall
%.2fx %s
##cam_aspect
Aspect
Multiplier of the native aspect ratio.
>1 = stretched (wider), <1 = squished.
##cam_aspect_w
Aspect width
##cam_aspect_h
Aspect height
